# scuttle M3.6: Chat Files Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** List a chat's files with `/files`, save one to the local filesystem from that list or with a click on its `attached` line, view a text file in the pager, jump to the message that carries a file, show an agent's attachment while the turn still streams, hold the `/chats` archive confirmation still while its choices change, keep the `/chats` selection beside a chat that leaves the list, complete workspace names after `/workspace`, and draw `/model` at the `/chats` size with each model's compaction threshold to change.

**Architecture:** The SDK gains one hand-written streaming download, `Client::download_chat_file`, because the generated `get_chat_file` drops the body.
`scuttle-core` gains `files.rs` (the file rows, safe names, and save types), a `file` live block, the `files.save_dir` key, and the `/files` command with its save, view, and jump flow and the "name is taken" question, all as messages and effects.
`scuttle-tui` downloads and writes files in `runtime.rs` through a hidden partial file that `save.rs` moves into place without replacing anything, draws the `/files` table and the question, and makes the `attached` line clickable.

**Tech Stack:** Rust 2024 on the toolchain in `rust-toolchain.toml` (1.98), ratatui 0.30, crossterm 0.29, tokio 1, reqwest 0.13 (in the SDK), toml 0.9, toml_edit 0.23, `coder-sdk` and its generated `coder-api-gen` client on `m3-6-sdk` (branched from `m3-sdk` at `af56c6c`), wiremock and portable-pty in tests.

**Spec:** Item 60 in `docs/feedback/2026-10-01-m2-daily-use.md`, in the author's words: "File downloads also don't appear/I'm not sure of a good mechanism for users to save those files locally... What might be good is a `/files` command that shows the history of files for that specific chat, as well as a per-message mechanism to save the file to the user's filesystem."
Item 61, from the same review: the archive confirmation in `/chats` jumps up and down as the selection moves, because a warning appears under the delete choice and the box re-centers, and a long chat title runs into the box's top-right border.
The author wants the same safety without the movement.
Item 62: after an archive in `/chats`, the selection jumps to the top; it should land on the chat above the archived one, else on the first chat.
Item 64, from the author: "I'd also like to add workspace autocomplete when typing `/workspace`".
Item 65, from the author: "the `/model` window should be almost full screen, same size as the `/chats` window, and in that window, I should also be able to change the default compaction percentage per model."
It also covers one item queued during M3.5: `config::config_path` accepts a relative `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, which `state::state_path` already ignores.

**Task order:** S1, S1b, Tasks 1 to 7, then Task 9, then Task 10, then Task 8, which checks the finished branch.

## Global Constraints

- scuttle work happens on branch `m3-6-files`, created from `m3-polish` at `3a3ec93`, in its own worktree.
- SDK work happens on a new branch `m3-6-sdk`, branched from `m3-sdk` at `af56c6c`, in its own worktree `.worktrees/m3-6-sdk` under the `unofficial-coder-sdk-rs` checkout's root.
  The `m3-sdk` branch and its worktree are never touched: `main` pins `branch = "m3-sdk"`, so a new commit there would reach any unlocked build or `cargo update` on `main`.
- No implementer pushes, and nobody commits on `main`, adds a remote, or stages a file a task does not name; never `git add -A`, because the worktree holds uncommitted documents that are not part of any task.
- The author approved pushing SDK work that leaves the stable release alone.
  The only push in this plan is S1b, which the controller runs once S1's review passes: it pushes `m3-6-sdk` and commits scuttle's `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` pinned to that branch by its GitHub URL.
  No file in this plan, committed or not, ever names the SDK by a `file:` URL or a local path.
- Only S1b changes the `coder-sdk` dependency line and its `Cargo.lock` pin, so from S1b on every commit builds from a clean checkout with `cargo build --workspace --locked`, and no later task stages `Cargo.toml` or `Cargo.lock`.
  If S1 needs a fix after S1b, the controller pushes the fix to `m3-6-sdk` and commits a fresh `cargo update -p coder-sdk`.
- No task adds a crate to any `Cargo.toml` or to `Cargo.lock`.
- `scuttle-core` has no terminal dependencies (no ratatui, crossterm, arboard, or tokio) and makes no network request.
- API calls and process launches happen only in `crates/scuttle-tui/src/runtime.rs`, or in a terminal handoff the main loop in `crates/scuttle-tui/src/main.rs` runs with the input thread paused (the pager, and `$EDITOR` through `Tui::open_editor` and `Tui::edit_settings`). `crates/scuttle-tui/src/save.rs` only touches the local filesystem.
- The session token is never printed, logged, rendered, or written anywhere by scuttle, and every SDK error the runtime turns into a `Msg` goes through its `Redactor`.
- A download sends the token only in the `Coder-Session-Token` header of `GET /api/v2/chats/files/{file}`; scuttle never calls the signed-URL endpoints, so no token ever appears in a URL.
  The download request sets its own 5 minute timeout with `RequestBuilder::timeout`, in place of the shared client's 60 second total, so a 10 MiB file on a slow link does not fail at 60 seconds.
- The local config file never holds secrets; this plan adds exactly one key, `files.save_dir`, which does not match the secret-name check in `config.rs`.
- A saved file is at most 10 MiB (`scuttle_core::attachments::MAX_FILE_BYTES`), is written to a hidden `.scuttle-<uuid>.part` file in the directory it is saved to, created with mode 0644 before the umask (the usual mode of a file a user downloads: readable by everyone, writable by its owner, never executable), and reaches its name only through a hard link that fails when the name is taken, an exclusive create where the file system has no hard links, or, only after the user chose "Replace", a rename. A failed or cancelled save leaves no partial file while scuttle runs; a kill without unwinding, such as `SIGKILL`, can leave the hidden `.scuttle-<uuid>.part` file behind.
- A server-supplied file name is never used as a path: `files::safe_name` takes its last component, strips control characters and the invisible format characters U+200B to U+200F, U+202A to U+202E, U+2066 to U+2069, and U+FEFF, replaces `<>:"|?*`, refuses `.` and `..`, and falls back to `attachment.<ext>`.
- scuttle never hands a downloaded file to the system opener or executes it.
- `open_in_browser` in `crates/scuttle-tui/src/runtime.rs` stays exactly as it is, and no task adds a `cfg!(test)` branch.
- Every config and state write goes through `write_config`: a temp file with mode 0600 moved into place, refusing a file its owner made read-only.
- Every crate sets `publish = false`, edition 2024, and the toolchain pinned in `rust-toolchain.toml`.
- Commit messages use Conventional Commits and end with the trailer `Assisted-by: AI`; they never name an AI model or vendor.
- A commit scope is a crate name (`scuttle-core`, `scuttle-tui`, `coder-sdk`), or no scope when a commit spans crates.
- Markdown files use one sentence per line and no em dashes, en dashes, or spaced double hyphens as punctuation; the same rule applies to code comments and user-facing strings.
- No coworker names, Linear IDs, real chat data, or absolute home-directory paths in any committed file. Test fixtures use synthetic ids, names, and paths.
- The existing insta snapshots must not change; if one does, the task broke M1 rendering.
- New tests never sleep to let time pass: TUI tests pass explicit `Instant`s where a clock matters, and runtime and pty tests wait on a channel or the screen with a timeout.
- No test reads the real environment or writes under the real home directory: every save in a test goes to a fresh directory under `std::env::temp_dir()`, and `home` is passed in.
- Run `cargo fmt --all` before each commit; every task ends green on `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo fmt --all --check`.
- M2's interfaces stay as they are: the core owns every request generation, replies keep their `Msg::ForChat`, `Msg::ForRefresh`, `Msg::ForOrg`, and `Msg::ForPlan` tagging, every terminal handoff pauses the input thread (`terminal::Input::pause` and `resume`), the TUI rebuilds `View` only when `view_revision` moved or the width or history reset changed, scroll anchoring stays in `keep_top` and `keep_marks`, and an effect that needs the terminal is answered by `Effect::runs_in_main`.
- M2.5's interfaces stay as they are: `Tui::new(config, config_path, theme, welcome, seed)`, a footer field a command changes reads as `/command: value`, `Tui::config` follows what `config.toml` holds, `/info` keeps `App::info_panel` and its `CostState`, and the wheel never scrolls the transcript under an overlay, the help box, or the question menu.
- M3's interfaces stay as they are: `Runtime::new(client, token, tx)`, `Tui::key(key, now)`, `Tui::usage_deadline` and `Tui::poll_usage`, `StatusField`'s names and `StatusField::DEFAULT`, the core's limit and cost generations, `IconSet::resolve` and the two-cell `icons::slot`, and `Effect::SendMessage` carrying an MCP selection through `send_chat_message_with_mcp_servers`.
- M3.5's interfaces and rulings stay as they are: `state::state_path` and `LocalState`, `Welcome::tip`, the organization MCP list in `Msg::ForOrg`, one wheel tick acting as one Up or Down in a table overlay, and the archive confirmation's safety rules (it opens on "Archive", Enter arms the delete and only `d` or `D` confirms it, any other key, move, or wheel tick disarms, `KeyEventKind::Repeat` is ignored, the workspace id is captured when it opens, and the in-flight guard runs before the archived check).
- Crate APIs in this plan were checked against the sources of ratatui 0.30.2, crossterm 0.29.0, tokio 1.53.1, reqwest 0.13.5, toml 0.9.12, toml_edit 0.23.10, wiremock, and the generated client on `m3-sdk`, but not compiled.
  When a signature differs in the resolved version, adapt the implementation and the test setup, keep every test assertion, and report the adaptation.

## Review Focus

- A hostile or careless stored name, such as `../../.ssh/authorized_keys`, `/etc/passwd`, `..\x`, `..`, a name with control characters or bidi controls such as U+202E, or a 300-byte name: the file must land inside the save directory under a cleaned basename of at most 255 bytes, never anywhere else (Task 3, `a_server_name_never_escapes_the_directory`, and Task 6, `a_hostile_name_saves_inside_the_directory`).
- A name already taken, by a file, a dangling symlink, or a symlink to a file elsewhere: nothing is replaced or written through without an explicit "Replace", "Replace" replaces the link and never its target, and a held Enter on the question can never pick "Replace" (Task 6, `a_taken_name_is_never_replaced_without_asking`, `replace_replaces_a_symlink_and_never_its_target`, and Task 7, `the_save_question_holds_the_keyboard_and_a_held_enter_never_replaces`).
- The "name is taken" box keeps one size and place on every choice, its title stays inside the border, and neither a paste nor a wheel tick reaches what it covers (Task 6, `the_save_question_keeps_its_place_and_size_on_every_choice` and `the_save_question_keeps_pastes_and_the_wheel_from_what_it_covers`).
- A download that is refused, cut off, or larger than 10 MiB, with or without a `Content-Length`: no partial file is left behind, and the error names the file and never the token (Task 6, `a_body_past_the_limit_without_a_length_leaves_nothing` and `a_refused_download_names_the_file_and_never_the_token`).
- A file whose message is older than the loaded history, as on a chat whose first page ends with `has_more`: `/files` still lists it from `chat.files` as "not loaded", and `g` loads older pages until the message shows up, or says it is not in the history; a jump in flight is dropped when the chat changes (Task 5, `jump_pages_older_history_until_the_message_loads_or_says_it_is_gone`, and Task 4, `leaving_the_chat_drops_a_jump_in_flight`).
- A text file that holds terminal escape sequences, viewed with `v`: control characters other than line breaks and tabs are removed before the pager sees it, so the file cannot drive the terminal (Task 5, `view_pages_text_without_control_characters_and_refuses_other_types`).
- `/workspace ` with nothing after the space, then the send key: it runs `/workspace` bare, as before, and never attaches the most recently used workspace unasked (Task 9, `the_send_key_takes_the_highlighted_argument_once_one_is_typed_or_picked`).
- Right held on a `/model` row, then Enter or Esc: the edit saves with one request, before the pick's own effects, never one per press (Task 10, `several_fast_presses_save_once_when_the_row_is_left` and `enter_still_picks_the_model_and_saves_its_edit`).
- A threshold save the server refuses, or a reply that lands after a newer edit: the row shows the value the server holds, and a stale reply changes nothing (Task 10, `a_failed_threshold_save_says_so_and_shows_the_server_value_again` and `a_stale_threshold_reply_never_overwrites_a_newer_edit`).
- The S1b pin: from S1b on, `Cargo.toml` names `m3-6-sdk` by its GitHub URL, `Cargo.lock` pins the S1 commit, nothing names a `file:` URL or a local path, and a clean clone builds with `--locked` (S1b, and Task 8).

## How the server and the web UI behave

These are the facts the tasks below match, cited at `d1597a583b`.

- **Message parts.** An attachment is a `file` part with `media_type`, an optional `name`, and `file_id` ([`ChatMessagePart`](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/codersdk/chats.go#L417-L420)); `StripInternal` clears inline `data` whenever `file_id` is set ([codersdk/chats.go#L514-L524](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/codersdk/chats.go#L514-L524)).
  On the author's deployment the legacy `file_name` arrives empty, so scuttle reads `name` first.
- **Agent attachments.** The `attach_file` tool takes `name` and `path` and stores the workspace file as a chat file ([attachfile.go#L24-L78](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/coderd/x/chatd/chattool/attachfile.go#L24-L78)); an empty `name` falls back to the path's last segment ([attachment.go#L76-L112](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/coderd/x/chatd/chattool/attachment.go#L76-L112)).
  While the turn streams, `publishToolAttachments` publishes each attachment as an assistant `message_part` with a `file` part, through the same publisher, so it takes the next `seq` ([chatloop.go#L1617-L1642](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/coderd/x/chatd/chatloop/chatloop.go#L1617-L1642)).
  The durable copy is an assistant message that holds the `file` part ([message_conversion.go#L162-L177](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/coderd/x/chatd/message_conversion.go#L162-L177)).
- **Names are untrusted.** The stored name is only normalized: control characters stripped, whitespace trimmed, cut to 255 bytes ([`NormalizeStoredFileName`](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/coderd/x/chatfiles/mime.go#L109-L136)), so separators and `..` survive, from a model's `attach_file` `name` or an uploader's `Content-Disposition`.
- **Limits.** Each file is at most 10 MiB ([`MaxChatFileSizeBytes`](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/codersdk/chats.go#L55-L57)), and a chat links at most 50 files by default, evicting the oldest past the cap ([codersdk/chats.go#L42-L44](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/codersdk/chats.go#L42-L44), [coderd/exp_chats.go#L7385-L7400](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/coderd/exp_chats.go#L7385-L7400)).
- **Download.** `GET /api/v2/chats/files/{file}` returns the bytes to a session-authenticated caller ([`chatFileByID`](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/coderd/exp_chats.go#L6696-L6730)), with `Content-Type`, `Content-Length`, and `Content-Disposition` built by `mime.FormatMediaType` ([`serveChatFile`](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/coderd/exp_chats.go#L6732-L6752)).
  It answers 404 for a file that is gone.
  The route is declared `@Success 200` with no schema, which is why the generated client returns `ResponseValue<()>`.
- **Listing.** No endpoint lists a chat's files alone; `GET /api/v2/chats/{chat}` carries `files`, every linked file's `id`, `name`, `mime_type`, `size_bytes`, and `created_at` ([codersdk/chats.go#L275-L285](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/codersdk/chats.go#L275-L285), [chatfiles.sql#L20-L29](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/coderd/database/queries/chatfiles.sql#L20-L29)).
  It does not say which message referenced a file, so "from" and "message" come from scanning the loaded messages.
- **Web UI.** A download is a plain link to the session-authenticated URL ([`getChatFileURL`](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/site/src/pages/AgentsPage/utils/chatAttachments.ts#L13-L14)), named by `getAttachmentDownloadName`, which adds an extension from the media type and falls back to `attachment.<ext>` ([AttachmentBlocks.tsx#L49-L155](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/site/src/pages/AgentsPage/components/ChatConversation/AttachmentBlocks.tsx#L49-L155)).
  It has no files list, and treats a referenced file missing from `chat.files` and older than the newest linked one as expired ([messageHelpers.ts#L315-L341](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/site/src/pages/AgentsPage/components/ChatConversation/messageHelpers.ts#L315-L341)).
  PDF and SVG are served as `attachment` because browsers run them as documents ([`IsInlineRenderableStoredMediaType`](https://github.com/coder/coder/blob/d1597a583b/coderd/x/chatfiles/mime.go#L73-L84)); scuttle opens no saved file at all.

## Decisions this plan makes that the feedback left open

- **Where files go.** `files.save_dir` in `config.toml`, with `~/` expanded.
  Left out, files go to `~/Downloads`, or to the home directory when there is no `Downloads` directory, or to the current directory with no home at all.
  Over SSH, the saved notice says the file is on the machine scuttle runs on.
- **A taken name.** scuttle checks before downloading, and the move into place checks again.
  A box asks "Keep both", "Replace", or "Cancel", starting on "Keep both", which saves as `name (1).ext`, or the next free number.
  "Replace" needs a move to it and Enter; a held Enter's repeats are ignored.
  The box follows the archive box's rule: every choice is one line with its own short description, cut with an ellipsis to the box, the risk shows on the "Replace" label itself, and the title is cut to fit inside the border, so the box keeps one size and place.
- **The per-message mechanism.** The transcript has only click targets today (`HitTarget::Toggle` and `HitTarget::CopyCode`) and no keyboard row selection; code blocks reach the keyboard through `/copy n`.
  So the smallest design follows that pattern: the `attached` line gains a `HitTarget::SaveFile` and reads `attached build-logs.zip · click to save` while scuttle takes the mouse and the chat still stores the file, and the keyboard route is `/files`, which opens on the newest file, so saving the latest attachment is `/files`, Enter.
- **`/files`.** A table overlay, newest first, not filterable, so its letter keys work: Enter saves, `s` saves under another path (`~/` expands; a directory saves inside it), `v` shows a text file in scuttle's own pager, and `g` closes the table and scrolls to the file's message.
  Its columns are name, type, size, from (`you` or `agent`), message (the turn's first words, `this turn` while streaming, or `not loaded`), and when.
  A file a message names but the chat no longer stores is dimmed and marked `(expired)`; only `g` works on it.
  `g` on a file in the turn that is still streaming scrolls to the end of the transcript, where that turn is.
- **Mode 0644.** Before the umask, as a browser download gets; a stricter umask such as 077 gives 0600.
- **The archive box (item 61).** Its size and place depend only on the terminal and on whether the chat has a workspace, both fixed while it is open, because every row and the hint are one line: the warning rows go, the delete choice's own label carries `(can't be undone)` in the error style, and the hint changes in place.
  A narrow box shortens the workspace name with an ellipsis before the flag, and the title is cut with an ellipsis to fit inside the border.
- **Workspace names (item 64).** The names match by prefix with an exact name first, as the slash menu matches commands, not through nucleo-matcher, which serves only the table filters.
  The send key takes the highlighted name once part of one is typed or Up or Down moved the highlight; after `/workspace ` alone it runs `/workspace` bare.
  Esc clears the line, as it does on the command menu.
- **`/model` (item 65).** It takes the `/chats` area through one shared `overlay_area`, and its rows show the context window, the compaction threshold in effect, and the reasoning efforts under each provider's row.
  Left and Right step the highlighted model's threshold by 5 within 0 and 100, Delete restores the default, and the edit saves once the selection leaves the row or the table closes; Task 10 gives the reasons.

### Task S1: Stream a chat file download (SDK)

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/coder-sdk/src/files.rs`
- Modify: `crates/coder-sdk/src/lib.rs` (`mod files;`, `pub use files::ChatFileDownload;`)
- Modify: `crates/coder-sdk/src/stream.rs` (`MAX_REFUSAL_BODY` and `capped_body` become `pub(crate)`)
- Test: `crates/coder-sdk/src/files.rs` (`mod tests`)

All of S1 happens in the SDK checkout, in a new worktree `.worktrees/m3-6-sdk` on a new branch `m3-6-sdk`; it touches no scuttle file.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Client::http()`, `Client::base_url()`, `Client::error_from_status(&self, status: u16, body: &[u8]) -> Error` (existing); `stream::capped_body(response: reqwest::Response, limit: usize) -> Vec<u8>` and `stream::MAX_REFUSAL_BODY` (made `pub(crate)` here).
- Produces:
  - `pub struct coder_sdk::ChatFileDownload { pub media_type: Option<String>, pub file_name: Option<String>, pub size: Option<u64>, /* private response */ }`.
  - `ChatFileDownload::chunk(&mut self) -> coder_sdk::Result<Option<Vec<u8>>>`.
  - `Client::download_chat_file(&self, file: uuid::Uuid) -> coder_sdk::Result<ChatFileDownload>`; a non-200 is the redacted `Error::from_status` (`Error::Unauthorized` on 401, `Error::Api { status, .. }` otherwise).
    Its request sets its own timeout, `files::DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT` (5 minutes), with `RequestBuilder::timeout`, which overrides the shared client's 60 second total for this request only.
  - `Client::download_request(&self, file: uuid::Uuid) -> coder_sdk::Result<reqwest::Request>` (private), the request `download_chat_file` sends.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing tests**

Create `crates/coder-sdk/src/files.rs` with only its tests:

```rust
//! Downloading a chat file, which the generated client cannot do.

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use secrecy::SecretString;
    use wiremock::matchers::{header, method, path};
    use wiremock::{Mock, MockServer, ResponseTemplate};

    use super::{DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT, disposition_name};
    use crate::{Client, Error, Session};

    const TOKEN: &str = "s3cr3t-session-token-do-not-leak";

    fn client(url: &str) -> Client {
        Client::new(&Session {
            url: url.parse().unwrap(),
            token: SecretString::from(TOKEN),
        })
        .unwrap()
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_file_downloads_in_chunks_with_the_token_only_in_a_header() {
        let server = MockServer::start().await;
        let file = uuid::Uuid::new_v4();
        let body: Vec<u8> = (0..200_000u32).map(|i| (i % 251) as u8).collect();
        Mock::given(method("GET"))
            .and(path(format!("/api/v2/chats/files/{file}")))
            .and(header("Coder-Session-Token", TOKEN))
            .respond_with(
                ResponseTemplate::new(200)
                    .set_body_bytes(body.clone())
                    .insert_header("content-type", "application/zip")
                    .insert_header(
                        "content-disposition",
                        "attachment; filename=\"build; logs.zip\"",
                    ),
            )
            .expect(1)
            .mount(&server)
            .await;
        let mut download = client(&server.uri()).download_chat_file(file).await.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(download.media_type.as_deref(), Some("application/zip"));
        assert_eq!(download.file_name.as_deref(), Some("build; logs.zip"));
        assert_eq!(download.size, Some(body.len() as u64));
        let mut got = Vec::new();
        while let Some(chunk) = download.chunk().await.unwrap() {
            got.extend_from_slice(&chunk);
        }
        assert_eq!(got, body);
        let requests = server.received_requests().await.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(requests[0].url.query(), None, "nothing rides in the URL");
        assert!(!requests[0].url.as_str().contains(TOKEN));
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_missing_file_is_a_404_api_error() {
        let server = MockServer::start().await;
        let file = uuid::Uuid::new_v4();
        Mock::given(path(format!("/api/v2/chats/files/{file}")))
            .respond_with(
                ResponseTemplate::new(404)
                    .set_body_json(serde_json::json!({"message": "Resource not found."})),
            )
            .mount(&server)
            .await;
        match client(&server.uri()).download_chat_file(file).await {
            Err(Error::Api {
                status, message, ..
            }) => {
                assert_eq!(status, 404);
                assert_eq!(message, "Resource not found.");
            }
            other => panic!("expected a 404, got {other:?}"),
        }
    }

    #[tokio::test]
    async fn a_refusal_redacts_the_token_and_a_rejected_token_is_unauthorized() {
        let server = MockServer::start().await;
        let (refused, rejected) = (uuid::Uuid::new_v4(), uuid::Uuid::new_v4());
        Mock::given(path(format!("/api/v2/chats/files/{refused}")))
            .respond_with(ResponseTemplate::new(403).set_body_json(serde_json::json!({
                "message": format!("bad token {TOKEN}"),
                "detail": format!("echoed {TOKEN}"),
            })))
            .mount(&server)
            .await;
        Mock::given(path(format!("/api/v2/chats/files/{rejected}")))
            .respond_with(ResponseTemplate::new(401))
            .mount(&server)
            .await;
        let c = client(&server.uri());
        let err = c.download_chat_file(refused).await.unwrap_err();
        let rendered = format!("{err} {err:?}");
        assert!(rendered.contains("[redacted]"), "{rendered}");
        assert!(!rendered.contains(TOKEN), "{rendered}");
        assert!(matches!(
            c.download_chat_file(rejected).await,
            Err(Error::Unauthorized)
        ));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_download_has_its_own_five_minute_timeout() {
        let request = client("http://127.0.0.1:1")
            .download_request(uuid::Uuid::nil())
            .unwrap();
        assert_eq!(DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT, std::time::Duration::from_secs(300));
        assert_eq!(
            request.timeout(),
            Some(&DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT),
            "the shared 60 second total would cut off a 10 MiB file on a slow link"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_disposition_name_is_read_quoted_or_bare() {
        assert_eq!(
            disposition_name("inline; filename=\"a \\\"b\\\" c.png\"").as_deref(),
            Some("a \"b\" c.png")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            disposition_name("attachment; FILENAME=notes.txt; size=3").as_deref(),
            Some("notes.txt")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            disposition_name("attachment; filename*=utf-8''caf%C3%A9.txt"),
            None,
            "the extended form is left to the chat's own record"
        );
        assert_eq!(disposition_name("inline"), None);
        assert_eq!(disposition_name("inline; filename=\"\""), None);
        assert_eq!(disposition_name("inline; filename=\"cut"), None);
    }
}
```

In `crates/coder-sdk/src/stream.rs`, change `const MAX_REFUSAL_BODY` to `pub(crate) const MAX_REFUSAL_BODY` and `async fn capped_body` to `pub(crate) async fn capped_body`.

Put this above the tests in `crates/coder-sdk/src/files.rs`, replacing its one-line doc comment:

```rust
//! Downloading a chat file, which the generated client cannot do: the server declares no
//! body for `GET /api/v2/chats/files/{file}` (`@Success 200`, `coderd/exp_chats.go`,
//! `chatFileByID`), so the generated `get_chat_file` returns `ResponseValue<()>` and drops the
//! bytes. The download here sends the session token in its header, as every request does, and
//! never uses the signed-URL endpoints, which put a token in the URL.

use std::time::Duration;

use reqwest::header::{CONTENT_DISPOSITION, CONTENT_TYPE, HeaderName};

use crate::stream::{MAX_REFUSAL_BODY, capped_body};
use crate::{Client, Error, Result};

/// How long a download may take, from connecting to its last byte. The shared client's 60
/// second total covers the body too, which a 10 MiB file on a link slower than about
/// 170 KiB/s would outlast, so a download sets its own.
pub(crate) const DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(5 * 60);

/// A chat file on its way, read a chunk at a time so the caller can write each piece out
/// rather than hold the whole file.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct ChatFileDownload {
    /// The stored media type, from `Content-Type`.
    pub media_type: Option<String>,
    /// The stored name, from `Content-Disposition`. A person or a model chose it, so it is
    /// not safe to use as a path.
    pub file_name: Option<String>,
    /// The body's length, from `Content-Length`, when the server sent one.
    pub size: Option<u64>,
    response: reqwest::Response,
}

impl ChatFileDownload {
    /// The next piece of the body, or `None` at its end.
    pub async fn chunk(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>> {
        Ok(self.response.chunk().await?.map(|bytes| bytes.to_vec()))
    }
}

/// The value of header `name` in `response`, when it is text.
fn header(response: &reqwest::Response, name: HeaderName) -> Option<String> {
    response
        .headers()
        .get(name)
        .and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok())
        .map(str::to_owned)
}

/// The `filename` parameter of a `Content-Disposition` value, quoted (with `\` escapes, as
/// Go's `mime.FormatMediaType` writes it) or bare. `None` when it is missing, empty, or cut
/// off, or when only the extended `filename*` form is present.
pub(crate) fn disposition_name(value: &str) -> Option<String> {
    const KEY: &str = "filename=";
    // Lowercasing changes only ASCII letters, so byte offsets stay the same in `value`.
    let lower = value.to_ascii_lowercase();
    let start = lower
        .match_indices(KEY)
        .map(|(i, _)| i)
        .find(|&i| i == 0 || matches!(lower.as_bytes()[i - 1], b';' | b' ' | b'\t'))?;
    let rest = &value[start + KEY.len()..];
    let name = match rest.strip_prefix('"') {
        Some(quoted) => {
            let mut name = String::new();
            let mut chars = quoted.chars();
            loop {
                match chars.next()? {
                    '"' => break,
                    '\\' => name.push(chars.next()?),
                    c => name.push(c),
                }
            }
            name
        }
        None => rest.split(';').next().unwrap_or_default().trim().to_owned(),
    };
    (!name.is_empty()).then_some(name)
}

impl Client {
    /// The request `download_chat_file` sends: a `GET` with its own `DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT`, which
    /// overrides the shared client's total for this request only.
    fn download_request(&self, file: uuid::Uuid) -> Result<reqwest::Request> {
        let url = self
            .base_url()
            .join(&format!("/api/v2/chats/files/{file}"))
            .map_err(|e| Error::Transport(e.to_string()))?;
        Ok(self.http().get(url).timeout(DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT).build()?)
    }

    /// Starts downloading chat file `file`. The session token goes only in the request's
    /// header, which the shared client adds. A refusal's text has the token redacted, as every
    /// hand-built request's does, and its body is read only up to a small cap.
    pub async fn download_chat_file(&self, file: uuid::Uuid) -> Result<ChatFileDownload> {
        let request = self.download_request(file)?;
        let response = self.http().execute(request).await?;
        let status = response.status().as_u16();
        if status != 200 {
            let body = capped_body(response, MAX_REFUSAL_BODY).await;
            return Err(self.error_from_status(status, &body));
        }
        let media_type = header(&response, CONTENT_TYPE);
        let file_name = header(&response, CONTENT_DISPOSITION)
            .as_deref()
            .and_then(disposition_name);
        let size = response.content_length();
        Ok(ChatFileDownload {
            media_type,
            file_name,
            size,
            response,
        })
    }
}
```

A refusal goes through error_from_status, so the token is redacted, and its body is capped.
```rust
    /// The archive box's top-left corner and size on `shown`: the `┌` before its title, and
    /// the `┐` and `└` that close it.
    fn archive_box(shown: &str) -> (usize, usize, usize, usize) {
        let lines: Vec<Vec<char>> = shown.lines().map(|l| l.chars().collect()).collect();
        let top = lines
            .iter()
            .position(|l| l.iter().collect::<String>().contains(" Archive \u{201c}"))
            .expect("the archive box shows");
        let title_at = lines[top]
            .iter()
            .collect::<String>()
            .find(" Archive \u{201c}")
            .unwrap();
        let title_col = lines[top].iter().collect::<String>()[..title_at].chars().count();
        let left = lines[top][..title_col]
            .iter()
            .rposition(|c| *c == '┌')
            .unwrap();
        let right = left
            + lines[top][left..]
                .iter()
                .position(|c| *c == '┐')
                .unwrap();
        let bottom = (top + 1..lines.len())
            .find(|&y| lines[y].get(left) == Some(&'└'))
            .unwrap();
        (left, top, right - left + 1, bottom - top + 1)
    }
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the fitting helpers and the new view**

In `crates/scuttle-tui/src/overlay.rs`, after `const CANCEL_ROW: &str = "cancel";`, add:

```rust
/// What the delete choice's label ends with, so the risk shows on the row itself.
const UNDONE_FLAG: &str = "(can't be undone)";

/// `text` cut to `max` display columns, with an ellipsis in place of what was cut.
pub(crate) fn ellipsize(text: &str, max: usize) -> String {
    use unicode_width::UnicodeWidthChar;
    if text.width() <= max {
        return text.to_owned();
    }
    let mut out = String::new();
    let mut used = 0;
    for c in text.chars() {
        let w = c.width().unwrap_or(0);
        if used + w + 1 > max {
            break;
        }
        out.push(c);
        used += w;
    }
    out.push('\u{2026}');
    out
}

/// The first of `forms` that fits in `max` columns, else the last, which is the shortest.
fn first_fit(forms: Vec<String>, max: usize) -> String {
    let last = forms.last().cloned().unwrap_or_default();
    forms.into_iter().find(|f| f.width() <= max).unwrap_or(last)
}

/// The archive box's title within a box `width` wide: `table::render` puts a space on each
/// side of it, inside the two corners, so a long chat title is cut with an ellipsis there.
fn archive_title(title: &str, width: u16) -> String {
    let room = usize::from(width).saturating_sub(4);
    let fixed = "Archive \u{201c}\u{201d}?".width();
    if room > fixed {
        format!("Archive \u{201c}{}\u{201d}?", ellipsize(title, room - fixed))
    } else {
        ellipsize("Archive", room)
    }
}
```

Replace `ArchivePrompt::workspace_phrase` with:

```rust
    /// How the confirmation names the workspace, with its name cut to `room` columns.
    fn workspace_phrase_within(&self, room: usize) -> String {
        match (&self.workspace_name, self.workspace) {
            (Some(name), _) => format!("workspace \u{201c}{}\u{201d}", ellipsize(name, room)),
            // The workspace list does not hold it, so the id is all there is to name it by.
            (None, Some(id)) => format!("workspace {}", &id.to_string()[..8]),
            (None, None) => "the chat's workspace".into(),
        }
    }

    /// How the confirmation names the workspace, whole.
    fn workspace_phrase(&self) -> String {
        self.workspace_phrase_within(usize::MAX)
    }

    /// The delete choice's label within `max` columns. The workspace's name gives way before
    /// the flag does, and when nothing else fits the flag leads, so it is never cut.
    fn delete_label(&self, max: usize) -> String {
        let label = |lead: &str, room: usize| {
            format!("{lead}{} {UNDONE_FLAG}", self.workspace_phrase_within(room))
        };
        let fitted = |lead: &str| {
            // With no room, the name is a lone ellipsis, one column wide.
            let fixed = label(lead, 0).width().saturating_sub(1);
            label(lead, max.saturating_sub(fixed).max(1))
        };
        first_fit(
            vec![
                fitted("Archive and delete "),
                fitted("Delete "),
                format!("{UNDONE_FLAG} Archive and delete"),
            ],
            max,
        )
    }
```

Replace `archive_view` and `render_archive_prompt` with:

```rust
/// The archive confirmation's rows for a box `width` wide. Every row and the hint are one
/// line, so moving between the choices or arming the delete never changes the box's size.
pub fn archive_view(prompt: &ArchivePrompt, theme: &Theme, width: u16) -> TableView {
    // Inside the border, and after the selection mark every row leaves room for.
    let row_room = usize::from(width).saturating_sub(4);
    let hint_room = usize::from(width).saturating_sub(2);
    let mut rows = vec![Row::item(
        RowKey::Action(ARCHIVE_ROW),
        vec![Line::from("Archive")],
    )];
    if prompt.workspace.is_some() {
        rows.push(Row::item(
            RowKey::Action(DELETE_ROW),
            vec![Line::from(Span::styled(
                prompt.delete_label(row_room),
                theme.error,
            ))],
        ));
    }
    rows.push(Row::item(
        RowKey::Action(CANCEL_ROW),
        vec![Line::from("Cancel")],
    ));
    let deleting = prompt.table.selected == Some(RowKey::Action(DELETE_ROW));
    // Shortened by dropping words, never by dropping a key's name.
    let hint = match (deleting, prompt.armed) {
        (_, true) => first_fit(
            vec![
                format!(
                    "Press D to delete {} for good, Esc cancels",
                    prompt.workspace_phrase()
                ),
                "Press D to delete for good, Esc cancels".into(),
                "D deletes, Esc cancels".into(),
            ],
            hint_room,
        ),
        (true, false) => first_fit(
            vec![
                "Up and Down choose, Enter arms the delete, Esc cancels".into(),
                "Enter arms the delete, Esc cancels".into(),
                "Enter arms, Esc cancels".into(),
            ],
            hint_room,
        ),
        (false, false) => first_fit(
            vec![
                "Up and Down choose, Enter confirms, Esc cancels".into(),
                "Enter confirms, Esc cancels".into(),
            ],
            hint_room,
        ),
    };
    TableView {
        title: archive_title(&prompt.title, width),
        widths: vec![Constraint::Fill(1)],
        rows,
        hint: Some(hint),
        hint_alarm: prompt.armed,
        filterable: false,
        ..Default::default()
    }
}

/// Where the archive confirmation draws in `area`: centered, at most `ARCHIVE_WIDTH` wide,
/// and as tall as its one-line rows and hint. It depends only on `area` and on whether the
/// chat has a workspace, both fixed while the box is open.
pub fn archive_rect(area: Rect, prompt: &ArchivePrompt, theme: &Theme) -> Rect {
    let width = area.width.min(ARCHIVE_WIDTH);
    let height = table::height(&archive_view(prompt, theme, width), width).min(area.height);
    Rect {
        x: area.x + (area.width - width) / 2,
        y: area.y + (area.height - height) / 2,
        width,
        height,
    }
}

/// Draws `prompt` as a box in the middle of `area`, over the `/chats` rows.
pub fn render_archive_prompt(f: &mut Frame, area: Rect, prompt: &ArchivePrompt, theme: &Theme) {
    let rect = archive_rect(area, prompt, theme);
    let view = archive_view(prompt, theme, rect.width);
    table::render(f, rect, &view, &prompt.table, theme, None);
}
```

In `ChatsState::archive_key`, the two `archive_view(prompt, ctx.theme)` calls become `archive_view(prompt, ctx.theme, ARCHIVE_WIDTH)`; they read only the rows' keys, which no width changes.

### Task 2: Keep the selection near a row that leaves a table

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/scuttle-tui/src/table.rs` (`TableState::above`, `TableState::adopted`, `TableState::index`, `TableState::remember_above`, `TableState::step`, `render`)
- Test: `crates/scuttle-tui/src/table.rs` and `crates/scuttle-tui/src/app.rs` (`mod tests`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `TableState`, `TableView`, `Row::selectable` (existing).
- Produces:
  - `TableState::above: RefCell<Option<RowKey>>`, the selectable row above the selected one, as last drawn or moved to.
  - `TableState::adopted: RefCell<Option<RowKey>>`, the row the selection fell back to once the selected row left, which stands in for it until the selection moves.
  - `TableState::index(&self, view)` keeps its signature: the selected row, else the row it adopted, else the remembered row above, else the first selectable row, else `None`.
  - `TableState::remember_above(&self, view: &TableView)`: while the selected row is in `view`, it remembers the row above and clears `adopted`; once it is gone, the selection adopts the row it falls back to and remembers the row above that one.

Why the selection jumps today: `TableState` keeps the selection by key (`selected: Option<RowKey>`), which is right, but `TableState::index` falls back to the first selectable row whenever that key is missing from the view.
An archived chat leaves the All, Active, and Unread tabs once `App::mark_archived` hands the list a `deleted` watch event, so the next draw finds no `RowKey::Chat` for it and selects the top row.
The same happens when another client archives the selected chat, or when an unarchived chat leaves the Archived tab.
The fix is at that root: the table remembers, by key, the selectable row above the selected one each time it draws or the selection moves, and falls back to it before the first row.
So it covers a plain archive, an archive that also deletes the workspace (which archives first, so a failed delete still removes the row), an unarchive in the Archived tab, and any refresh that drops the selected row, in every table overlay.
A refused archive keeps the chat, so its key still matches and nothing moves.
The neighbor is found by id wherever a refresh moved it, and the ratatui offset in `TableState::offset` is kept, so the viewport does not jump to the top.
The neighbor is the one above the chat when it was last drawn, which is when the confirmation showed over it; a refresh that reorders the rows before the archive lands updates it to the row above in the new order, which is still the row the user saw above it.
Once the selection falls back, it adopts that row, and the next draw remembers the row above the adopted one, so archiving the row the fallback chose hands the selection to the row above it in turn, rather than to the first row.

```rust
    fn view_of(keys: &[i64]) -> TableView {
        TableView {
            rows: keys
                .iter()
                .map(|k| Row::item(RowKey::Queued(*k), vec![Line::from(k.to_string())]))
                .collect(),
            ..Default::default()
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_row_that_leaves_hands_the_selection_to_the_row_above_it() {
        let mut state = TableState::with_selected(RowKey::Queued(2));
        state.remember_above(&view_of(&[1, 2, 3]));
        let after = view_of(&[3, 1]);
        assert_eq!(state.index(&after), Some(1), "found by key after a reorder");
        let first = TableState::with_selected(RowKey::Queued(1));
        first.remember_above(&view_of(&[1, 2, 3]));
        assert_eq!(first.index(&view_of(&[2, 3])), Some(0), "no row above: the first");
        assert_eq!(first.index(&view_of(&[])), None, "nothing to select");
        let kept = view_of(&[1, 2, 3]);
        assert_eq!(state.index(&kept), Some(1), "a row that stays keeps the selection");
        state.handle_key(
            crossterm::event::KeyEvent::new(KeyCode::Down, KeyModifiers::NONE),
            &kept,
        );
        assert_eq!(
            state.index(&view_of(&[1, 2])),
            Some(1),
            "a move remembers the new row above without a draw"
        );
        let mut moved = TableState::with_selected(RowKey::Queued(2));
        moved.remember_above(&view_of(&[1, 2, 3]));
        moved.selected = Some(RowKey::Queued(9));
        moved.remember_above(&view_of(&[1, 2, 3]));
        assert_eq!(
            moved.index(&view_of(&[3, 1])),
            Some(1),
            "a selection that is already missing falls back to key 1, the row above key 2"
        );

        let state = TableState::with_selected(RowKey::Queued(4));
        state.remember_above(&view_of(&[1, 2, 3, 4, 5]));
        let without_4 = view_of(&[1, 2, 3, 5]);
        assert_eq!(state.index(&without_4), Some(2), "the row above 4");
        state.remember_above(&without_4);

            state.index(&view_of(&[1, 2, 5])),

        state.remember_above(&view_of(&[1, 2, 5]));
```

```rust
    /// `/chats` open on idle roots titled by `titles`, newest first, in `query`'s list.
    fn titled_chats_tui(titles: &[&str], query: scuttle_core::chat_list::ListQuery) -> (Tui, Vec<uuid::Uuid>) {
        let mut t = tui();
        started(&mut t);
        let ids: Vec<uuid::Uuid> = titles.iter().map(|_| uuid::Uuid::new_v4()).collect();
        let archived = query == scuttle_core::chat_list::ListQuery::Archived;
        let chats = serde_json::from_value(json!(titles
            .iter()
            .zip(&ids)
            .enumerate()
            .map(|(i, (title, id))| json!({"id": id, "title": title, "status": "waiting",
                "archived": archived, "updated_at": format!("2026-09-30T{:02}:00:00Z", 20 - i),
                "children": [], "files": [], "mcp_server_ids": [], "inline_mcp_servers": [],
                "labels": {}}))
            .collect::<Vec<_>>()))
        .unwrap();
        t.core.update(Msg::ChatsLoaded {
            query,
            offset: 0,
            chats,
        });
        show(&mut t, vec![Effect::ShowChats(String::new())]);
        (t, ids)
    }

    /// The chat `/chats` selects once drawn.
    fn selected_chat(t: &mut Tui) -> Option<uuid::Uuid> {
        screen(t, 100, 24);
        let (view, _, _) = t.overlay_view.as_ref()?;
        match t.overlay.as_ref()?.state().selected_row(view).map(|r| r.key.clone()) {
            Some(table::RowKey::Chat(id)) => Some(id),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Archives the selected chat through the confirmation, and lands the server's answer.
    fn archive_selected(t: &mut Tui, chat: uuid::Uuid) {
        t.handle(ctrl_a());
        screen(t, 100, 24);
        assert!(!enter(t).is_empty(), "the archive was asked for");
        t.update(Msg::ChatUpdated {
            chat,
            change: scuttle_core::app::ChatChange::Archived(true),
        });
    }

    #[test]
    fn archiving_a_row_selects_the_row_above_it_or_the_first() {
        use scuttle_core::chat_list::ListQuery;
        let (mut t, ids) = titled_chats_tui(&["A", "B", "C", "D", "E"], ListQuery::Default);
        for _ in 0..3 {
            t.handle(key(KeyCode::Down, KeyModifiers::NONE));
        }
        assert_eq!(selected_chat(&mut t), Some(ids[3]));
        archive_selected(&mut t, ids[3]);
        assert_eq!(selected_chat(&mut t), Some(ids[2]), "the row above D");
        // C is the row the fallback chose, and B is above it, while A is the first row.
        archive_selected(&mut t, ids[2]);
        assert_eq!(selected_chat(&mut t), Some(ids[1]), "the row above C, not the first");
        archive_selected(&mut t, ids[1]);
        assert_eq!(selected_chat(&mut t), Some(ids[0]), "the row above B");
        archive_selected(&mut t, ids[0]);
        assert_eq!(selected_chat(&mut t), Some(ids[4]), "no row above: the new first");
        archive_selected(&mut t, ids[4]);
        assert_eq!(selected_chat(&mut t), None, "nothing left to select");
    }

            chat: ids[1],
            change: scuttle_core::app::ChatChange::Archived(false),
        });

    }
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Remember the row above**

In `crates/scuttle-tui/src/table.rs`, change `use std::cell::Cell;` to `use std::cell::{Cell, RefCell};`, and add to `TableState` after `offset`:

```rust
    /// The selectable row above the selected one when it was last drawn or moved to, by key.
    /// When the selected row leaves the view, as an archived chat leaves `/chats`, the
    /// selection falls back to it instead of jumping to the top.
    pub above: RefCell<Option<RowKey>>,
    /// The row the selection fell back to once the selected row left the view, by key. It
    /// stands in for the selected row until the selection moves, so when it leaves in turn,
    /// the selection goes to the row above it rather than to the top.
    pub adopted: RefCell<Option<RowKey>>,
```

Replace `TableState::index` with:

```rust
    /// The index of the selected row; else, once it left the view, of the row the selection
    /// adopted; else of the row that was above; else of the first selectable row.
    pub fn index(&self, view: &TableView) -> Option<usize> {
        let find = |key: &RowKey| view.rows.iter().position(|r| r.selectable() && &r.key == key);
        let first = || view.rows.iter().position(Row::selectable);
        match self.selected.as_ref() {
            Some(key) => find(key)
                .or_else(|| self.adopted.borrow().as_ref().and_then(find))
                .or_else(|| self.above.borrow().as_ref().and_then(find))
                .or_else(first),
            None => first(),
        }
    }

    /// Remembers the selectable row above the selected one in `view`. Once the selected row
    /// is missing from `view`, the selection adopts the row it falls back to, and the row
    /// above that one is remembered instead.
    pub fn remember_above(&self, view: &TableView) {
        let Some(key) = self.selected.as_ref() else {
            return;
        };
        let at = match view.rows.iter().position(|r| r.selectable() && &r.key == key) {
            Some(at) => {
                *self.adopted.borrow_mut() = None;
                at
            }
            None => {
                let Some(at) = self.index(view) else {
                    return;
                };
                *self.adopted.borrow_mut() = Some(view.rows[at].key.clone());
                at
            }
        };
        *self.above.borrow_mut() = view.rows[..at]
            .iter()
            .rev()
            .find(|r| r.selectable())
            .map(|r| r.key.clone());
    }
```

`index` takes its borrows of `adopted` and `above` and drops them before `remember_above` writes either one.

At the end of `step`, after `self.selected = Some(view.rows[selectable[next]].key.clone());`, add `self.remember_above(view);`.
In `render`, right after `let index = state.index(view);`, add `state.remember_above(view);`.

```rust
            ("shot", "image/png; charset=binary", "shot.png"),
            ("invoice\u{202e}gpj.zip", "application/zip", "invoicegpj.zip"),
            ("\u{feff}a\u{200b}b\u{2066}.txt", "text/plain", "ab.txt"),
        ] {
            assert_eq!(safe_name(name, kind), want, "{name:?} as {kind}");
        }
        let long = format!("{}.pdf", "a".repeat(300));
        let cut = safe_name(&long, "application/pdf");
        assert!(cut.len() <= MAX_NAME_BYTES && cut.ends_with(".pdf"), "{cut}");
        let wide = format!("{}.txt", "\u{e9}".repeat(200));
        let cut = safe_name(&wide, "text/plain");
        assert!(cut.len() <= MAX_NAME_BYTES && cut.ends_with(".txt"), "{cut}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_kept_copy_takes_the_next_number_before_its_extension() {
        assert_eq!(numbered("report.pdf", 1), "report (1).pdf");
        assert_eq!(numbered("README", 2), "README (2)");
        let long = numbered(&format!("{}.pdf", "a".repeat(251)), 7);
        assert!(long.len() <= MAX_NAME_BYTES && long.ends_with(" (7).pdf"), "{long}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn types_have_a_badge_and_say_whether_they_show() {
        assert_eq!(badge("application/zip"), "ZIP");
        assert_eq!(badge("image/svg+xml"), "SVG");
        assert_eq!(badge("application/x-thing"), "FILE");
        assert_eq!(extension("image/jpeg"), Some("jpg"));
        assert!(is_text("text/csv") && is_text("application/json") && is_text("TEXT/PLAIN"));
        assert!(!is_text("image/png") && !is_text("application/zip"));
        assert_eq!(printable("a\u{1b}]52;c;eA==\u{7}b\r\n\tc"), "a]52;c;eA==b\n\tc");
    }

    #[test]
    fn paths_expand_and_show_the_home_directory_as_a_tilde() {
        let home = Path::new("/h");
        assert_eq!(expand_home("~/out/a.txt", Some(home)), PathBuf::from("/h/out/a.txt"));
        assert_eq!(expand_home("~", Some(home)), PathBuf::from("/h"));
        assert_eq!(expand_home("~/x", None), PathBuf::from("~/x"));
        assert_eq!(expand_home("/tmp/x", Some(home)), PathBuf::from("/tmp/x"));
        assert_eq!(display_path(Path::new("/h/Downloads/a"), Some(home)), "~/Downloads/a");
        assert_eq!(display_path(Path::new("/h"), Some(home)), "~");
        assert_eq!(display_path(Path::new("/tmp/a"), Some(home)), "/tmp/a");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_save_target_joins_only_the_safe_name_to_a_directory() {
        let is_dir = |p: &Path| p == Path::new("/dl") || p == Path::new("/typed");
        assert_eq!(
            SaveTo::Dir("/dl".into()).target("a.txt", is_dir),
            PathBuf::from("/dl/a.txt")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            SaveTo::Typed("/typed".into()).target("a.txt", is_dir),
            PathBuf::from("/typed/a.txt"),
            "a typed directory saves inside it"
        );
        assert_eq!(
            SaveTo::Typed("/typed/b.txt".into()).target("a.txt", is_dir),
            PathBuf::from("/typed/b.txt"),
            "a typed file name is used as typed"
        );
        assert_eq!(
            SaveTo::File("/dl".into()).target("a.txt", is_dir),
            PathBuf::from("/dl"),
            "an exact path is never joined"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn rows_list_every_file_newest_first_with_who_sent_it_and_where() {
        let chat = chat(json!([
            {"id": OLD, "name": "notes.md", "mime_type": "text/markdown", "size_bytes": 120,
                "created_at": "2026-10-05T11:00:00Z"},
            {"id": FILE, "name": "build-logs.zip", "mime_type": "application/zip",
                "size_bytes": 6144, "created_at": "2026-10-05T12:00:02Z"}
        ]));
        let t = transcript(json!([
            {"id": 3, "role": "user", "created_at": "2026-10-05T12:00:00Z", "content": [
                {"type": "text", "text": "  \nbundle the build logs for me please, thanks"}]},
            {"id": 4, "role": "assistant", "created_at": "2026-10-05T12:00:01Z", "content": [
                {"type": "tool-call", "tool_call_id": "c1", "tool_name": "attach_file",
                    "args": {"name": "build-logs.zip", "path": "/workspace/build-logs.zip"}}]},
            {"id": 6, "role": "assistant", "created_at": "2026-10-05T12:00:02Z", "content": [
                {"type": "file", "file_id": FILE, "media_type": "application/zip",
                    "name": "build-logs.zip", "file_name": ""}]},
            {"id": 7, "role": "user", "created_at": "2026-10-05T12:01:00Z", "content": [
                {"type": "file", "file_id": FILE, "media_type": "application/zip",
                    "name": "build-logs.zip"}]}
        ]));
        let rows = file_rows(Some(&chat), &t);
        assert_eq!(rows.len(), 2);
        assert_eq!(rows[0].id, id(FILE), "newest first");
        assert_eq!(rows[0].name, "build-logs.zip");
        assert_eq!(rows[0].media_type, "application/zip");
        assert_eq!(rows[0].size, Some(6144));
        assert_eq!(rows[0].from, Some(Sender::Agent), "its first message wins");
        assert_eq!(
            rows[0].place,
            Place::Message {
                id: 6,
                label: "\u{201c}bundle the build logs fo\u{2026}\u{201d}".into()
            }
        );
        assert!(!rows[0].expired);
        assert_eq!(rows[1].id, id(OLD));
        assert_eq!(rows[1].from, None, "its message is older than the loaded history");
        assert_eq!(rows[1].place, Place::NotLoaded);
        assert_eq!(message_with(&t, id(FILE)), Some(6));
        assert_eq!(message_with(&t, id(OLD)), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_file_the_chat_dropped_is_expired_and_a_live_one_is_listed() {
        let chat = chat(json!([
            {"id": FILE, "name": "new.png", "mime_type": "image/png", "size_bytes": 10,
                "created_at": "2026-10-05T12:00:00Z"}
        ]));
        let mut t = transcript(json!([
            {"id": 2, "role": "user", "created_at": "2026-10-05T10:00:00Z", "content": [
                {"type": "text", "text": "here"},
                {"type": "file", "file_id": GONE, "media_type": "text/plain", "name": "a\u{7}.txt"}]}
        ]));
        t.live.blocks.push(LiveBlock::File {
            file_id: Some(id(OLD)),
            name: Some("shot.png".into()),
            media_type: Some("image/png".into()),
        });
        let rows = file_rows(Some(&chat), &t);
        let gone = rows.iter().find(|r| r.id == id(GONE)).unwrap();
        assert!(gone.expired, "older than the newest stored file and not stored");
        assert_eq!(gone.name, "a.txt", "control characters never reach the screen");
        assert_eq!(gone.from, Some(Sender::You));
        assert_eq!(
            gone.place,
            Place::Message {
                id: 2,
                label: "\u{201c}here\u{201d}".into()
            }
        );
        let live = rows.iter().find(|r| r.id == id(OLD)).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(live.place, Place::Live);
        assert_eq!(live.from, Some(Sender::Agent));
        assert_eq!(rows[0].id, id(OLD), "a file with no time yet is the newest");
        assert!(file_rows(None, &Transcript::default()).is_empty());
    }
}
```

Put this above the tests in `crates/scuttle-core/src/files.rs`, replacing its one-line doc comment:

```rust
//! A chat's files: the rows `/files` lists, and the safe names they are saved under.
//!
//! `chat.files` names every file the chat still stores, with its size and time, but not the
//! message that carried it or who sent it; those come from the loaded messages, which may be
//! only the newest page. A stored name is untrusted: a person or a model chose it, and the
//! server keeps path separators and `..` in it.

use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

use coder_sdk::types;
use uuid::Uuid;

use crate::live::LiveBlock;
use crate::transcript::Transcript;

/// The longest file name most file systems take, in bytes.
pub const MAX_NAME_BYTES: usize = 255;

/// The most columns a message's first words take in `/files`.
const LABEL_CHARS: usize = 24;

/// Who sent a file into the chat.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Sender {
    You,
    Agent,
}

/// Where a file's message is.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Place {
    /// A loaded message, by id, with the first words of its turn.
    Message { id: i64, label: String },
    /// The turn the agent is streaming now.
    Live,
    /// A message older than the loaded history.
    NotLoaded,
}

/// One row of `/files`.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct FileRow {
    pub id: Uuid,
    /// The stored name without control characters, for display. It is not a safe path.
    pub name: String,
    /// The base media type, lowercase, such as `image/png`; empty when unknown.
    pub media_type: String,
    pub size: Option<u64>,
    /// When it was stored, or when its message was sent, in seconds since the Unix epoch.
    pub created: Option<i64>,
    /// `None` while its message is not loaded, since `chat.files` does not say.
    pub from: Option<Sender>,
    pub place: Place,
    /// A message names it, but the chat no longer stores it, so it cannot be downloaded.
    pub expired: bool,
}

/// Where a save writes.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum SaveTo {
    /// The save directory, where the file takes its safe name.
    Dir(PathBuf),
    /// A path the user typed: inside it when it is a directory, else exactly there.
    Typed(PathBuf),
    /// Exactly this path, as a "Keep both" or "Replace" after a taken name.
    File(PathBuf),
}

impl SaveTo {
    /// The file a save of `name`, a `safe_name`, writes.
    pub fn target(&self, name: &str, is_dir: impl Fn(&Path) -> bool) -> PathBuf {
        match self {
            SaveTo::Dir(dir) => dir.join(name),
            SaveTo::Typed(path) if is_dir(path) => path.join(name),
            SaveTo::Typed(path) | SaveTo::File(path) => path.clone(),
        }
    }
}

/// What a save does when its name is taken.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum OnConflict {
    /// Asks, with `Msg::FileConflict`, and writes nothing.
    Ask,
    Replace,
    /// Saves under the next free `numbered` name.
    KeepBoth,
}

/// An answer to "the name is taken".
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ConflictChoice {
    KeepBoth,
    Replace,
    Cancel,
}

impl ConflictChoice {
    /// The choices in the order the question lists them; it starts on the first.
    pub const ALL: [ConflictChoice; 3] = [
        ConflictChoice::KeepBoth,
        ConflictChoice::Replace,
        ConflictChoice::Cancel,
    ];
}

/// A key for the "name is taken" question.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ConflictKey {
    Up,
    Down,
    Enter,
    Cancel,
}

/// A `/files` key, or a click on an attached file's line.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum FileAction {
    /// Saves to the save directory.
    Save(Uuid),
    /// Asks where to save it.
    SaveAs(Uuid),
    /// Shows a text file in the pager.
    View(Uuid),
    /// Scrolls the transcript to the message that carries it.
    Jump(Uuid),
}

impl FileAction {
    pub fn file(self) -> Uuid {
        match self {
            FileAction::Save(id)
            | FileAction::SaveAs(id)
            | FileAction::View(id)
            | FileAction::Jump(id) => id,
        }
    }
}

/// A save that found its name taken, waiting on the user's answer.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct SaveConflict {
    pub file: Uuid,
    /// The file's safe name, for the messages a retry shows.
    pub name: String,
    /// The taken path.
    pub path: PathBuf,
    pub choice: ConflictChoice,
}

fn kind(p: &types::CodersdkChatMessagePart) -> &str {
    p.type_.as_ref().map(|t| t.as_str()).unwrap_or_default()
}

/// A part's file name: `name`, else the legacy `file_name`, which servers now send empty.
fn part_name(p: &types::CodersdkChatMessagePart) -> &str {
    [&p.name, &p.file_name]
        .into_iter()
        .find_map(|n| n.as_deref().filter(|n| !n.trim().is_empty()))
        .unwrap_or_default()
}

/// Whether `c` is a control character, or an invisible format character that changes how a
/// name reads without showing itself: a zero-width space, joiner, or mark (U+200B to U+200F),
/// a bidi embedding or override (U+202A to U+202E), a bidi isolate (U+2066 to U+2069), or a
/// byte order mark (U+FEFF). `char::is_control` covers only the first kind, so
/// `invoice\u{202e}gpj.zip` would read as `invoicepiz.jpg`.
fn hidden(c: char) -> bool {
    c.is_control()
        || matches!(
            c,
            '\u{200b}'..='\u{200f}' | '\u{202a}'..='\u{202e}' | '\u{2066}'..='\u{2069}' | '\u{feff}'
        )
}

/// `name` without control or invisible format characters, trimmed, for the screen.
fn display_name(name: &str) -> String {
    name.chars()
        .filter(|c| !hidden(*c))
        .collect::<String>()
        .trim()
        .to_owned()
}

/// The name a row shows: its own, or a stand-in when it has none.
pub fn shown_name(name: &str) -> &str {
    if name.is_empty() { "the file" } else { name }
}

/// `media_type` without parameters, lowercase.
fn base_type(media_type: &str) -> String {
    media_type
        .split(';')
        .next()
        .unwrap_or_default()
        .trim()
        .to_ascii_lowercase()
}

/// The first words of `text`'s first non-blank line, quoted, cut to `LABEL_CHARS`.
fn label(text: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let line = text.lines().map(str::trim).find(|l| !l.is_empty())?;
    let mut words: String = line.chars().take(LABEL_CHARS).collect();
    if line.chars().count() > LABEL_CHARS {
        words.push('\u{2026}');
    }
    Some(format!("\u{201c}{words}\u{201d}"))
}

/// Every file of the chat, newest first: those `chat.files` stores, those the loaded
/// messages carry, and those the live turn streams. A file takes the first message that
/// carries it. One a message carries but the chat no longer stores, and older than the
/// newest stored one, is expired, as the web UI judges it.
pub fn file_rows(chat: Option<&types::CodersdkChat>, transcript: &Transcript) -> Vec<FileRow> {
    let stored = chat.map(|c| c.files.as_slice()).unwrap_or_default();
    let newest_stored = stored
        .iter()
        .filter_map(|f| f.created_at)
        .map(|t| t.timestamp())
        .max();
    let mut rows: Vec<FileRow> = stored
        .iter()
        .filter_map(|meta| {
            Some(FileRow {
                id: meta.id?,
                name: display_name(meta.name.as_deref().unwrap_or_default()),
                media_type: base_type(meta.mime_type.as_deref().unwrap_or_default()),
                size: meta.size_bytes.and_then(|s| u64::try_from(s).ok()),
                created: meta.created_at.map(|t| t.timestamp()),
                from: None,
                place: Place::NotLoaded,
                expired: false,
            })
        })
        .collect();
    let mut turn: Option<String> = None;
    for m in transcript.messages() {
        let from = match m.role.as_ref().map(|r| r.as_str()) {
            Some("user") => Sender::You,
            Some("assistant") => Sender::Agent,
            _ => continue,
        };
        if from == Sender::You
            && let Some(words) = m
                .content
                .iter()
                .filter(|p| kind(p) == "text")
                .find_map(|p| label(p.text.as_deref().unwrap_or_default()))
        {
            turn = Some(words);
        }
        let sent = m.created_at.map(|t| t.timestamp());
        for p in m.content.iter().filter(|p| kind(p) == "file") {
            let Some(id) = p.file_id else { continue };
            let place = Place::Message {
                id: m.id.unwrap_or_default(),
                label: turn.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| "earlier".into()),
            };
            match rows.iter_mut().find(|r| r.id == id) {
                Some(row) if row.from.is_none() => {
                    row.from = Some(from);
                    row.place = place;
                }
                Some(_) => {}
                None => rows.push(FileRow {
                    id,
                    name: display_name(part_name(p)),
                    media_type: base_type(p.media_type.as_deref().unwrap_or_default()),
                    size: None,
                    created: sent,
                    from: Some(from),
                    place,
                    expired: matches!((sent, newest_stored), (Some(s), Some(n)) if s < n),
                }),
            }
        }
    }
    for block in &transcript.live.blocks {
        let LiveBlock::File {
            file_id: Some(id),
            name,
            media_type,
        } = block
        else {
            continue;
        };
        match rows.iter_mut().find(|r| r.id == *id) {
            Some(row) if row.from.is_none() => {
                row.from = Some(Sender::Agent);
                row.place = Place::Live;
            }
            Some(_) => {}
            None => rows.push(FileRow {
                id: *id,
                name: display_name(name.as_deref().unwrap_or_default()),
                media_type: base_type(media_type.as_deref().unwrap_or_default()),
                size: None,
                created: None,
                from: Some(Sender::Agent),
                place: Place::Live,
                expired: false,
            }),
        }
    }
    // A file with no time yet, such as a live one, is the newest. The sort is stable.
    rows.sort_by_key(|r| std::cmp::Reverse(r.created.unwrap_or(i64::MAX)));
    rows
}

/// The id of the first loaded message that carries `file`.
pub fn message_with(transcript: &Transcript, file: Uuid) -> Option<i64> {
    transcript
        .messages()
        .find(|m| {
            m.content
                .iter()
                .any(|p| kind(p) == "file" && p.file_id == Some(file))
        })
        .and_then(|m| m.id)
}

/// The extension a file of `media_type` takes, for the types a chat stores.
pub fn extension(media_type: &str) -> Option<&'static str> {
    Some(match base_type(media_type).as_str() {
        "image/png" => "png",
        "image/jpeg" => "jpg",
        "image/gif" => "gif",
        "image/webp" => "webp",
        "image/svg+xml" => "svg",
        "text/plain" => "txt",
        "text/markdown" => "md",
        "text/csv" => "csv",
        "application/json" => "json",
        "application/pdf" => "pdf",
        "application/zip" => "zip",
        _ => return None,
    })
}

/// The short type `/files` shows, such as `PNG`, or `FILE` for a type it does not know.
pub fn badge(media_type: &str) -> String {
    extension(media_type).map_or_else(|| "FILE".into(), str::to_ascii_uppercase)
}

/// Whether `v` can show a file of `media_type` in the pager.
pub fn is_text(media_type: &str) -> bool {
    let t = base_type(media_type);
    t.starts_with("text/") || t == "application/json"
}

/// `text` without control characters other than line breaks and tabs, so a file cannot
/// send the terminal an escape sequence through the pager.
pub fn printable(text: &str) -> String {
    text.chars()
        .filter(|c| !c.is_control() || matches!(c, '\n' | '\t'))
        .collect()
}

/// `text` with a leading `~` or `~/` resolved against `home`.
pub fn expand_home(text: &str, home: Option<&Path>) -> PathBuf {
    match (text, home) {
        ("~", Some(home)) => home.to_owned(),
        (text, Some(home)) if text.starts_with("~/") => home.join(&text[2..]),
        (text, _) => PathBuf::from(text),
    }
}

/// `path` as notices show it, with `home` as `~`.
pub fn display_path(path: &Path, home: Option<&Path>) -> String {
    match home.and_then(|h| path.strip_prefix(h).ok()) {
        Some(rest) if rest.as_os_str().is_empty() => "~".into(),
        Some(rest) => format!("~/{}", rest.display()),
        None => path.display().to_string(),
    }
}

/// `text` cut to at most `bytes` bytes, on a character boundary.
fn cut(text: &str, bytes: usize) -> &str {
    let mut end = bytes.min(text.len());
    while !text.is_char_boundary(end) {
        end -= 1;
    }
    &text[..end]
}

/// `name`'s stem and extension, when it has a short extension after a non-empty stem.
fn split_ext(name: &str) -> Option<(&str, &str)> {
    name.rsplit_once('.')
        .filter(|(stem, ext)| !stem.is_empty() && !ext.is_empty() && ext.len() <= 10 && !ext.contains(' '))
}

/// `name` cut to `MAX_NAME_BYTES`, keeping its extension.
fn fit(name: &str) -> String {
    let (stem, tail) = match split_ext(name) {
        Some((stem, ext)) => (stem, format!(".{ext}")),
        None => (name, String::new()),
    };
    format!("{}{tail}", cut(stem, MAX_NAME_BYTES.saturating_sub(tail.len())))
}

/// A file name for `name`, a stored name, that is safe to join to a directory: its last
/// path component, without control or invisible format characters, with `<>:"|?*` replaced, without leading
/// dots or trailing dots and spaces, with the extension `media_type` takes when it has none,
/// and at most `MAX_NAME_BYTES`. A name with nothing left is `attachment.<ext>`, as the web
/// UI names it.
pub fn safe_name(name: &str, media_type: &str) -> String {
    let last = name.rsplit(['/', '\\']).next().unwrap_or_default();
    let cleaned: String = last
        .chars()
        .filter(|c| !hidden(*c))
        .map(|c| {
            if matches!(c, '<' | '>' | ':' | '"' | '|' | '?' | '*') {
                '_'
            } else {
                c
            }
        })
        .collect();
    let trimmed = cleaned
        .trim()
        .trim_start_matches('.')
        .trim_end_matches(['.', ' '])
        .trim();
    let ext = extension(media_type);
    if trimmed.is_empty() || trimmed == "." || trimmed == ".." {
        return match ext {
            Some(ext) => format!("attachment.{ext}"),
            None => "attachment".into(),
        };
    }
    match ext {
        Some(ext) if split_ext(trimmed).is_none() => fit(&format!("{trimmed}.{ext}")),
        _ => fit(trimmed),
    }
}

/// `name` with ` (n)` before its extension, for a copy kept beside a taken name.
pub fn numbered(name: &str, n: u32) -> String {
    let (stem, tail) = match split_ext(name) {
        Some((stem, ext)) => (stem, format!(" ({n}).{ext}")),
        None => (name, format!(" ({n})")),
    };
    format!("{}{tail}", cut(stem, MAX_NAME_BYTES.saturating_sub(tail.len())))
}
```

In `crates/scuttle-core/src/config.rs`, after `ChatsConfig`, add:

```rust
/// The `[files]` table: where chat files are saved.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Deserialize, Default)]
#[serde(default)]
pub struct FilesConfig {
    /// The directory Enter in `/files` and a click on an attached file save to; `~/` is the
    /// home directory. Left out, `save_dir` picks one.
    pub save_dir: Option<PathBuf>,
}

/// Where files are saved: `files.save_dir` with `~` expanded, else `~/Downloads` when it is a
/// directory, else the home directory, else the current directory.
pub fn save_dir(files: &FilesConfig, home: Option<&Path>, is_dir: impl Fn(&Path) -> bool) -> PathBuf {
    if let Some(dir) = files.save_dir.as_deref() {
        return crate::files::expand_home(&dir.to_string_lossy(), home);
    }
    match home {
        Some(home) if is_dir(&home.join("Downloads")) => home.join("Downloads"),
        Some(home) => home.to_owned(),
        None => PathBuf::from("."),
    }
}
```

Add `pub files: FilesConfig,` to `LocalConfig` after `pub chats: ChatsConfig,`, with the doc comment `/// Where chat files are saved.`, and `files: FilesConfig::default(),` to its `Default` after `chats: ChatsConfig::default(),`.


Replace `config_path` with:

```rust
/// `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/scuttle/config.toml`, else `$HOME/.config/scuttle/config.toml`. A
/// relative or empty `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` is ignored, as the XDG Base Directory spec requires
/// and as `state::state_path` does.
pub fn config_path(env: &dyn Fn(&str) -> Option<String>) -> Option<PathBuf> {
    let base = env("XDG_CONFIG_HOME")
        .map(PathBuf::from)
        .filter(|p| p.is_absolute())
        .or_else(|| env("HOME").map(|h| PathBuf::from(h).join(".config")))?;
    Some(base.join("scuttle").join("config.toml"))
}
```

### Task 4: The `/files` command, saves, views, and jumps (core)

**Files:**
- Modify: `crates/scuttle-core/src/commands.rs` (`Command::Files`, `COMMANDS`, `parse`, `every_listed_command_parses`)
- Modify: `crates/scuttle-core/src/app.rs` (`EditTarget::SaveAs`, `Msg` and `Effect` variants, `App` fields, `update` arms, `file_action`, `save_file`, `conflict_key`, `jump_to_file`, `resume_jump`, `validate_edit`, `finish_edit`, the `Command::Files` arm, the `OlderLoaded` and `OlderFailed` arms, `reset_chat_state`)
- Modify: `crates/scuttle-tui/src/runtime.rs` (the no-op arm of `run`, and a placeholder arm Task 5 replaces)
- Modify: `crates/scuttle-tui/src/app.rs` (a temporary `EditTarget::SaveAs` arm in `draw_editor`, which Task 6 fills)
- Test: `crates/scuttle-core/src/app.rs` and `crates/scuttle-core/src/commands.rs` (`mod tests`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: everything Task 3 produces in `scuttle_core::files`.
- Produces:
  - `Command::Files`, listed as `/files` after `/attach`.
  - `EditTarget::SaveAs(Uuid)`.
  - `Msg::FileAction(FileAction)`, `Msg::ConflictKey(ConflictKey)`, and the runtime's replies, never wrapped in `Msg::ForChat`, since a saved file stays saved whichever chat is open: `Msg::FileSaved { file: Uuid, path: PathBuf }`, `Msg::FileConflict { file: Uuid, name: String, path: PathBuf }`, `Msg::FileText { file: Uuid, text: String }`, `Msg::FileFailed { file: Uuid, message: String }`.
  - `Effect::ShowFiles`, `Effect::ScrollToMessage(i64)`, and `Effect::ScrollToLatest` (the end of the transcript, where the live turn is) for the UI; `Effect::SaveFile { file: Uuid, name: String, to: SaveTo, conflict: OnConflict }` and `Effect::ReadFile { file: Uuid, name: String }` for the runtime.
  - `App::save_dir: PathBuf`, `App::home: Option<PathBuf>`, `App::over_ssh: bool` (the UI sets them), `App::files_busy: BTreeSet<Uuid>`, and `App::save_conflict: Option<SaveConflict>`.
  - Private: `App::jump`, which `reset_chat_state` clears; `save_conflict` and `files_busy` stay when the chat changes, since a save outlives the chat.

No key or click hands a file to the system opener; `v` shows text in scuttle's own pager.
